D183 (Fri Jul 2) A Birthday for a Country
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The scissors are adult-only and the paper strips are all they cut today, so put them back out of reach between cuts. The rolled candles are the width of Kreston's throat. Count six onto the cake and six off, and count them again after every blowing round at the party, because the cake sits on a table and he is on the blanket below it. Never leave one on the floor while he is down. Cut grapes lengthways. The scissors stay with you.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: What a Party Needs] **🧰 You need:** 1 sheet of paper · crayons · her.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: What a Party Needs] 1. Ask her what a party has to have. Write the list in her order, one thing per line, with a small picture beside each.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: What a Party Needs] 2. Tell her the country has a birthday on Sunday and turns 251. Say the number twice and let her do what she likes with it.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: What a Party Needs] 3. Circle everything on the list you could make out of a cereal box. Whatever she circled first is what you build at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Cake That Never Gets Eaten] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Cake That Never Gets Eaten] 1. Tape the box shut along every seam and stand it on its end. She tapes, you hold the flaps down.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Cake That Never Gets Eaten] 2. She tears the colored paper up and tapes it over the box until no cardboard shows. Torn edges look like frosting. Plain paper gets crayoned first.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Cake That Never Gets Eaten] 3. Cut six paper strips, a hand long. She rolls each one round the pencil, slides it off and tapes it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Cake That Never Gets Eaten] 4. Draw a flame on the top of each candle with the marker, then write **251** big on the side.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Cake That Never Gets Eaten] 5. Poke six holes in the top with the pencil where she points and stand a candle in each, loose enough to fall over.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Cake That Never Gets Eaten] 6. Sing to it. She blows, you tip the candles flat, she stands them back up. As many times as she asks.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Counting the Flags on the Street] Go before the heat and walk the streets with the most porches. Count flags: on
    [🌳 Get Outside: Counting the Flags on the Street] poles, on porch rails, on car windows, in shop doorways. Take her morning list
    [🌳 Get Outside: Counting the Flags on the Street] and let her mark a line on the back for each one. She will want bunting to
    [🌳 Get Outside: Counting the Flags on the Street] count, so let it count.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Counting the Flags on the Street] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Guest Who Doesn't Know It's a Party] Kreston has never been to a party. Azlyn throws him one, and she runs it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Guest Who Doesn't Know It's a Party] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Guest Who Doesn't Know It's a Party] 1. She picks the room and spreads the blanket. Wherever it lands is where the party is.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Guest Who Doesn't Know It's a Party] 2. Teddies round the edge, Kreston in the ring with them. She hands out the six cups and decides who sits by who.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Guest Who Doesn't Know It's a Party] 3. Music on. She carries the cake in from another room and stands it on a low table at the edge of the blanket, above Kreston's reach, while everybody watches her do it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Guest Who Doesn't Know It's a Party] 4. Sing. She blows the candles down and stands them back up, and you sing again. Four rounds is not too many, and every candle that rolls off the table gets counted back into the cake before the next one.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Guest Who Doesn't Know It's a Party] 5. She shares the snack into the cups, one guest at a time, in the order she chose.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Guest Who Doesn't Know It's a Party] 6. She runs the dancing. The same song twice, and you on the floor for it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Guest Who Doesn't Know It's a Party] 7. The party ends when she says. Cups collected in, cake up on the shelf where Kreston cannot reach the candles.
D184 (Sat Jul 3) The Parade She Can Ride In
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Streamers near the front wheel will wrap and stop it dead, so check the front is clear before every ride. Helmet on, and stay between her and the road at every driveway.     [🌅 Opening Activity: Cutting the Streamers] **🧰 You need:** 2 plastic grocery bags · 1 pair of scissors (**adult only**) · 1 roll masking tape.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Cutting the Streamers] 1. Cut the handles and the base off one bag so it becomes a tube. She holds it taut and pulls each strip free.

    [🌅 Opening Activity: Cutting the Streamers] 3. Tape a bundle to a chair back and let her run past to see them lift. The best bundle goes on the front at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Dressing the Scooter] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**

    [🎨 The Main Event: Dressing the Scooter] 6. Park it by the front door. It stays dressed until Sunday night.

    [🌳 Get Outside: The Test Lap] rehearsal, so let her stop every time a card comes loose. July mornings are
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Test Lap] warm by half ten: shaded side, and carry water.

    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Museum with an Empty Shelf] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Museum with an Empty Shelf] 1. Walk the house hunting for the oldest things in it, asking at each one: is this older than you? The wrong answers make the best conversations.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Museum with an Empty Shelf] 2. Line them up on the shelf in her order, oldest first, and leave that order alone.

  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Lit candles come nowhere near a walking one-year-old. Put Kreston in the high chair before the matches come out, not on your hip. July heat is the real risk: water every half hour whether she asks or not. Before dark, photograph what she is wearing and write your phone number on her arm.

    [🎨 The Main Event: Two Hundred and Fifty-One Candles] 2. Pull the green tops off the strawberries and let her slice them. Fruit gives under a blunt knife and she needs to feel that.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Two Hundred and Fifty-One Candles] 3. Stripes of strawberry across the cake with cream showing between them, blueberries in a square in the top left. She places every berry.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Two Hundred and Fifty-One Candles] 4. She pushes her candles in wherever she wants them, including all in one corner.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Two Hundred and Fifty-One Candles] 5. Light them once now, sing, and let her blow. Blow out the rest yourself and put the matches back up high.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Two Hundred and Fifty-One Candles] 6. Stand it beside Friday's cardboard cake and photograph the pair. One gets eaten tonight and one does not.

    [🌳 Get Outside: The Parade Round the Block] the cards rattling. Wave at anyone on a porch, because on the Fourth they wave

    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bag for After Dark] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**

D189 (Thu Jul 8) Three Rules
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Coins are a choking hazard for a 13-month-old who walks over and picks things up, so the purse lives in her pocket or your hand and the change goes straight back into it. The market means traffic, a crowd and a curb: say the three rules before you get out of the car, not on the sidewalk, and keep a hand on the stroller brake while she pays.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: What Makes It a Game] **🧰 You need:** 1 ball · a clear bit of floor.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: What Makes It a Game] 1. Play a game she knows, rolling the ball between you, and stop halfway through on purpose.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: What Makes It a Game] 2. Play it again with no rules for one minute: ball anywhere, score whenever you like, both at once. Let it get daft and let her complain.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: What Makes It a Game] 3. Ask what was wrong with that. Her answer is what you build the new game out of.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Game That Goes on the Fridge] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Game That Goes on the Fridge] 1. Ask her to invent a game and let it run long and mad. Take every rule she says, including the ones about dragons.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Game That Goes on the Fridge] 2. Play it once through as she described it. It falls apart somewhere, and that collapse is the material for the hour.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Game That Goes on the Fridge] 3. Cut it down to three. Say them back as a list: *you have to do this, you can't do that, this is how you win.* Let her correct you until it matches.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Game That Goes on the Fridge] 4. Write the three rules on the card in marker, with a picture beside each so she can read them back without you.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Game That Goes on the Fridge] 5. Take the chalk out and mark what the game needs on the ground: a start line, a target, a place to stand.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Game That Goes on the Fridge] 6. Tape the card to the fridge at her height. It stays there for the summer and anyone can play it.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Chalking the Pitch] Take the chalk and the ball to the biggest flat surface you can reach on foot
    [🌳 Get Outside: Chalking the Pitch] and draw the game out full size: the line, the target, a box for each player.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Chalking the Pitch] Play it five times, and anyone walking past gets asked to join.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Chalking the Pitch] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Money in Her Own Pocket] The market is at half two and she is going in with three coins that belong to
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Money in Her Own Pocket] her and a list she can read.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Money in Her Own Pocket] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Money in Her Own Pocket] 1. Decide together what she is buying. Three things, and she draws each one on the paper however she likes.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Money in Her Own Pocket] 2. Write the word beside each drawing in marker, so a stallholder can read it over her shoulder if she goes shy.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Money in Her Own Pocket] 3. Count the three coins into her hand, then into the purse. Open and shut it ten times, because a purse she fumbles gets handed to you.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Money in Her Own Pocket] 4. Agree the three market rules out loud in her words: hold the bag, hands off the fruit unless we are buying it, stay where you can see me.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Money in Her Own Pocket] 5. Rehearse the asking. You be the stallholder, four times, until *can I have one please* comes out without a run-up.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Money in Her Own Pocket] 6. She packs the bag herself: the list, the purse, nothing else.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Money in Her Own Pocket] 7. At the market she hands the money over and takes the change back into her purse while you stand behind her.
D190 (Fri Jul 9) Float It or Sink It
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Standing water. Kreston is 13 months, he walks, and he can drown in the depth of a dishpan in silence. D197 (Fri Jul 16) Ice Cream Without a Freezer
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Ice packed in salt drops well below freezing and burns bare skin faster than plain ice does. The parcel stays inside the dish towel and never rests against her arm. Put the back of your hand on the sunny slab before either of them stands on it. If you cannot hold it there, it comes off bare feet and Kreston stays on the shaded side. Loose cubes and spilled salt come off the floor straight away: Kreston walks at 13 months, and a cube he finds is a choking risk.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Filling the Cold Bag] **🧰 You need:** 1 large zip-seal freezer bag · a tray of ice cubes · 6 tbsp table salt · 1 tablespoon · masking tape and a marker.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Filling the Cold Bag] 1. She loads ice into the big bag by the handful until it is half full. It sticks to wet fingers and she will refuse help.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Filling the Cold Bag] 2. Six tablespoons of salt go in on top. She spoons, you count out loud.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Filling the Cold Bag] 3. Seal it, tape a strip across the front and write **COLD BAG** on it. Tell her the salt is doing something to that ice, and she finds out at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Ten Minutes of Shaking] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Ten Minutes of Shaking] 1. Pour the cream into the small bag. She adds the sugar and a drop of vanilla, and smells it first.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Ten Minutes of Shaking] 2. Press the bag flat on the table to push the air out, seal it and tape along the seal. Trapped air splits it later.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Ten Minutes of Shaking] 3. Open the cold bag, bury the small bag in the ice and salt, and seal it again.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Ten Minutes of Shaking] 4. Wrap the dish towel round the parcel and shake. Sing something with a long chorus, hand it over, take it back when her arms go.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Ten Minutes of Shaking] 5. At five minutes she presses a finger on the small bag through the towel. It sets at the edges before the middle.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Ten Minutes of Shaking] 6. At ten, lift it out, wipe the salt off with the towel, open it and eat out of the bag with the two spoons.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Ice Cube Race on the Step] Take four cubes out to the front step on a plate at half past ten, after three
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Ice Cube Race on the Step] hours of July sun on the paving. Two go on the hot slab, two in the shade of the
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Ice Cube Race on the Step] wall. The hot pair is water before you finish talking about it. Bare feet on
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Ice Cube Race on the Step] both slabs tell her why.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Ice Cube Race on the Step] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Keeping One Cube Till Dinner] Five ice cubes go into five wrappings at one o'clock, and one has to last until
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Keeping One Cube Till Dinner] dinner.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Keeping One Cube Till Dinner] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Keeping One Cube Till Dinner] 1. Tear five strips of tape, number them 1 to 5 with the marker, and she sticks one on each saucer.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Keeping One Cube Till Dinner] 2. Cube 1 sits bare. Cube 2 goes in the folded dish towel, cube 3 in foil, cube 4 buried in shredded newspaper inside the lidded shoebox, cube 5 into the bowl of water. Each stands on its numbered saucer.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Keeping One Cube Till Dinner] 3. Line them up out of the sun. Ask which wins, write all five numbers on a sixth strip of tape stuck to the wall and circle the one she picks to win.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Keeping One Cube Till Dinner] 4. Set the timer for twenty minutes. Each time it goes she opens all five and you cross the dead ones off the wall strip. Leave the kitchen in between, because the timer is what brings her back.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Keeping One Cube Till Dinner] 5. Around three, tell her where this comes from. Men sawed blocks out of frozen lakes in winter, packed them in straw inside a thick-walled ice house and sold that ice in August.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Keeping One Cube Till Dinner] 6. Whichever cube reaches six o'clock goes in a drink at the table, and she names the winner.
D198 (Sat Jul 17) The Ice Cream Shop
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The craft knife cuts the hatch and goes back out of reach between cuts, not onto the arm of the sofa. Check inside the box for staples before either child gets near it. Kreston climbs at 13 months, so weight the box down or lay it flat whenever he is loose and you are not beside him.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Six Flavors and a Sign] **🧰 You need:** 1 sheet of paper · crayons · 1 marker.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Six Flavors and a Sign] 1. Ask her for six flavors. Write them word for word and give the invented ones the same weight as the real ones.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Six Flavors and a Sign] 2. She draws a scoop beside each name in whatever color that flavor is to her.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Six Flavors and a Sign] 3. Read the six back in a shop voice, loud. That paper is the menu and it goes on the board at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Hatch in the Big Box] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Hatch in the Big Box] 1. Stand her behind the box and mark the hatch with tape at her chest height, wide enough for a tray. Cut it with her standing back, and say why.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Hatch in the Big Box] 2. Roll the ten sheets into cones. She rolls, you tape the seam, and the wonky ones stay in the pile.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Hatch in the Big Box] 3. Scrunch newspaper into balls, two scoops a cone. She will make forty of them.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Hatch in the Big Box] 4. Lay the flattened box down for the menu board and tape this morning's menu sheet to the middle of it. She copies the scoops bigger around the edge in crayon.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Hatch in the Big Box] 5. Cut the cardboard coins palm-sized, so nothing small reaches the floor. She draws a number on each.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Hatch in the Big Box] 6. Prop the board against the box, stand behind the hatch and buy something off her. Pay with a coin, wait for change.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Chalk Menu on the Path] Take the chalk to the sidewalk outside the front door and let her write the
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Chalk Menu on the Path] shop's name across it, the flavors underneath and prices she invents. Third
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Chalk Menu on the Path] week of July, so work in whatever shade the house throws. Anyone walking past
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Chalk Menu on the Path] gets the menu read at them.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Chalk Menu on the Path] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Jobs She Hands Out] The shop opens at one o'clock with staff, and the rule is that you take the job
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Jobs She Hands Out] she gives you.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Jobs She Hands Out] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Jobs She Hands Out] 1. Tuck the dish towel into her waistband as an apron. She rings the bell, or bangs the lid with the spoon, and the shop is open.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Jobs She Hands Out] 2. She hands out jobs: who scoops, who pays, who carries the tray. Take yours without negotiating.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Jobs She Hands Out] 3. Everybody queues with coins and orders off the board. She serves two paper scoops a cone, takes the money and hands back nonsense change. Let it be nonsense.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Jobs She Hands Out] 4. Swap over. She becomes the customer, you run a bad shop and get her order wrong.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Jobs She Hands Out] 5. Sit her down with the crayons and the marker to draw each person doing their job on the side of the box. Write the names where she says.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Jobs She Hands Out] 6. At half past three the real snack goes through the hatch on the tray, and the shop does a round with real food in it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Jobs She Hands Out] 7. Leave it standing. She is behind that counter again at quarter past four with nobody watching.
D199 (Sun Jul 18) The Sundae with Everybody In It
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** No nuts, no whole grapes and no hard sprinkles anywhere near Kreston at 13 months. His fruit is mashed and his portion is soft. Only pick what you can name. Anything you are not certain of stays on the bush, and nothing goes in Kreston's mouth unwashed. Jam loosened with hot water scalds, so it cools before she touches it. Plastic beakers rather than glass, and nothing sweet left at his height.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Pudding Joseph Remembers] **🧰 You need:** 1 sheet of paper · 1 pen · everybody who is in the house.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Pudding Joseph Remembers] 1. Ask Joseph what he ate on hot days when he was small. Let him take his time over the detail, because the detail is the part she repeats later.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Pudding Joseph Remembers] 2. She asks everyone else the same question in her own words.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Pudding Joseph Remembers] 3. Write the answers down with a name beside each. That list sits on the table until 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Glass in the House] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Glass in the House] 1. Stand the four beakers in a row. Everybody names one layer and she goes round asking. Write each name and layer on the card in order.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Glass in the House] 2. She breaks the bananas up with her hands. No knife, and the pieces will be huge.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Glass in the House] 3. Cookies into the bag, and she crushes them with her fist against the table.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Glass in the House] 4. Loosen the jam with a spoon of hot water yourself and leave it to cool. Cocoa stirred into sugar with a splash of hot water makes the chocolate sauce if there is none in.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Glass in the House] 5. Build all four in the order on the card, a scoop of ice cream under each new layer and the tinned fruit where the card says. Her hands, your steadying.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Glass in the House] 6. Photograph the card next to the beakers before anyone eats, then hand out the long spoons.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Berries for the Top] Walk to whatever fruit is ripe within stroller distance in the third week of July.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Berries for the Top] Raspberries on somebody's fence, currants gone dark, the first blackberries if
    [🌳 Get Outside: Berries for the Top] the summer has run warm. She picks into a tub, eats half of what she picks, and
    [🌳 Get Outside: Berries for the Top] the rest goes in the freezer for tomorrow's pots.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Berries for the Top] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tin by the Cookbooks] Clear the table after lunch and fill in four cards, then put them in a tin that
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tin by the Cookbooks] stays in this kitchen for years.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tin by the Cookbooks] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tin by the Cookbooks] 1. One card in front of her at a time. She answers out loud and you write it down.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tin by the Cookbooks] 2. Card one: what Joseph ate on hot days when he was small, from this morning. Card two: what gets made in this house at birthdays.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tin by the Cookbooks] 3. Card three: what Brooklyn's family ate that nobody makes now. Card four: the food she wants to be hers, the one people will say is her thing.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tin by the Cookbooks] 4. She draws on the back of each. Her drawing is the part she will recognize in ten years.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tin by the Cookbooks] 5. This morning's recipe card joins the pile.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tin by the Cookbooks] 6. Spread out any old photos to hand. She names who is in them and dictates a line about each onto a card.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tin by the Cookbooks] 7. Tape a label on the tin and write what she calls it. Photograph every card, then the tin goes on the shelf with the cookbooks.

  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Standing water is the hazard of the day. Ice cubes go in the water, never in his mouth.

    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Pots, Four Names] 4. She mashes each pot with the fork, one at a time, and the fork gets wiped in between or all four taste the same.

    [🎨 Second Main Event: Which One Is Yours] The pots come out at one o'clock and nobody is told which is theirs. Everybody
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Which One Is Yours] tastes with their eyes shut and guesses.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Which One Is Yours] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Which One Is Yours] 1. Bring the pots out and turn the lids face down so nobody reads a label early.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Which One Is Yours] 2. Everyone takes a turn with the dish towel over their eyes and a clean teaspoon, tasting all four.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Which One Is Yours] 3. A mouthful of water from the pitcher between tastes, paper towels for the chins.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Which One Is Yours] 4. Each person guesses which pot was made for them. She keeps score on her fingers and gets the count wrong.

D201 (Tue Jul 20) The Ice Cream Van
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** An adult keeps a hand on the stroller out on the sidewalk, whoever is doing the pushing, and the round stays this side of the curb. At the pool Kreston is within arm's reach every second he is in the water, and armbands are not a substitute for your hands. Clear the hall run before he pushes the basket: door frames, the corner of the shoe rack, anything hard at his head height, because he goes faster than he can stop.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Three Sounds the Van Makes] **🧰 You need:** 1 metal spoon · 1 saucepan lid · her voice.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Three Sounds the Van Makes] 1. Ask what an ice cream van sounds like. Get three sounds out of her: the chime, the engine idling, the hatch sliding open.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Three Sounds the Van Makes] 2. She makes each one with her mouth and you copy her, wrong enough that she corrects you.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Three Sounds the Van Makes] 3. Settle the chime: three hits of the spoon on the lid, the same three every time. That is the signal for the rest of the day.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Van That Comes Down Our Street] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Van That Comes Down Our Street] 1. Tape the flattened box to the front of the stroller or the side of the basket. She says what the sign says and you write it big.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Van That Comes Down Our Street] 2. Load the van: eight paper cups, the paper scoops, the tray laid across the top.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Van That Comes Down Our Street] 3. Chalk three stops on the floor if it is tile or lino, tape three crosses if it is carpet. She chooses where they go and one of them will be daft.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Van That Comes Down Our Street] 4. She pushes, you follow, and the chime rings the whole way. At each stop everybody stops dead and she serves whoever is standing there.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Van That Comes Down Our Street] 5. The rule for the round: moving while the chime rings, still when it stops. Vary how long you leave the silence.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Van That Comes Down Our Street] 6. Swap over. You push and she rings, which is the harder job, because the person holding the chime decides when everybody else moves.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Three Stops and a Bell] Take the round out to the sidewalk. Chalk three stops along the path: one at the
    [🌳 Get Outside: Three Stops and a Bell] gate, one further than she expects, one at the corner. Then walk the loop with
    [🌳 Get Outside: Three Stops and a Bell] the bell going. Twenty past ten in late July is warm enough that the tarmac
    [🌳 Get Outside: Three Stops and a Bell] smells of it. Serve a cup of water at every stop and let her ring at the
    [🌳 Get Outside: Three Stops and a Bell] neighbor's gate.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Three Stops and a Bell] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Passing the Cone on the Beat] Everybody sits down for this one. One paper cone goes round a circle on a beat
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Passing the Cone on the Beat] she controls by the end of it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Passing the Cone on the Beat] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Passing the Cone on the Beat] 1. Cushions in a circle on the floor, everyone sitting close enough to pass without leaning.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Passing the Cone on the Beat] 2. Tap the lid at walking pace. The cone moves one person per tap and nobody runs ahead of the beat.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Passing the Cone on the Beat] 3. Speed it up until it falls apart, then slow it down to something almost too slow to sit through. She calls fast or slow.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Passing the Cone on the Beat] 4. Music on the phone instead of the beat. The music stops and whoever holds the cone names a flavor, and no flavor twice.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Passing the Cone on the Beat] 5. Eyes-shut round. Dish towel over her eyes, and she passes and receives by feel while everyone stays quiet.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Passing the Cone on the Beat] 6. Hand her the bell. She sets the tempo and the whole circle moves to her hand, which she will test to destruction.

D203 (Thu Jul 22) The Treat That Takes All Day
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Hard chunks of ice are a choking hazard for Kreston at 13 months, so his portion is scraped fine and slushy and handed to him sitting down.     [🌅 Opening Activity: Sweet Enough to Freeze] 3. She tastes both and calls which is sweeter. Tell her sweet water freezes soft enough to scrape and plain water freezes into a brick, and the tray goes in at 9:15.

D206 (Sun Jul 25) Float, Sink, Squeeze
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Standing water and a 13-month-old who walks. The bowl and the pool get emptied the moment you stop watching, with no exception for a two-minute trip indoors. The sachet, the pen lid, the pebbles and the coins are all choking-sized and stay in your pocket when they are not in her hands. Never leave Kreston alone beside water, however shallow.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Water Above the Rim] **🧰 You need:** 1 clear 2-liter bottle with its cap · 1 pitcher · 1 bowl · 1 old towel.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Water Above the Rim] 1. Towel on the table, bowl on the towel, bottle in the bowl. She pours from the pitcher and the bowl catches what misses.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Water Above the Rim] 2. Fill it to the neck, then past it, until the water bulges above the rim. She will spill it, and that is what the bowl is for.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Water Above the Rim] 3. Cap on, tip it over and hunt for the bubble at the top. No bubble is the target, and she will fill it three times to get there.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Diver in the Bottle] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Diver in the Bottle] 1. Half fill the bottle from the pitcher and drop the sachet in. It wants to float with one corner breaking the surface. A sachet that sinks straight down is the wrong one.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Diver in the Bottle] 2. Top the bottle up over the bowl until the water stands proud of the rim, then screw the cap down hard.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Diver in the Bottle] 3. Squeeze the sides with both hands. The diver sinks. Let go and it rises. Do it once, then hand her the bottle.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Diver in the Bottle] 4. Stand back. She will run it forty times without help, and the towel is there because she hugs the bottle to her chest.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Diver in the Bottle] 5. Ask her to make it stop halfway, then to tell you what she is doing to it.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The River Down the Driveway] A pitcher of water tipped out at the top of the drive makes a river, and hot July
    [🌳 Get Outside: The River Down the Driveway] paving drinks it in four minutes. Race two pours down two lines. Drop a leaf in
    [🌳 Get Outside: The River Down the Driveway] and follow it to the curb. Ask her where the water goes once it leaves the
    [🌳 Get Outside: The River Down the Driveway] paving.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The River Down the Driveway] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Foil Boat and the Pebble Cargo] numbers go on the paving in chalk, and all three boats are the same shape in
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Foil Boat and the Pebble Cargo] three different sizes.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Foil Boat and the Pebble Cargo] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Foil Boat and the Pebble Cargo] 1. Stand the bowl in the shade and fill it from the pitcher. One towel down beside it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Foil Boat and the Pebble Cargo] 2. Tear three sheets of foil off the roll, a small one, a middling one and a big one. She pinches all three into the same deep cup shape, the one that carried most in June.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Foil Boat and the Pebble Cargo] 3. Chalk a big **1**, **2** and **3** on the paving and park the boats on their numbers, smallest first.

D210 (Thu Jul 29) Sea Water and Shade
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** A fingertip off the weak jar at 8:00 is the point of the activity. The dark plate in the sun goes up high too. Heat is the day's other hazard: water every half hour, shade by 11:00, hats outdoors, and everyone comes in if Azlyn goes quiet and flushed.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Stirring Until It Vanishes] **🧰 You need:** 1 clear jar · 1 pitcher of water · table salt · 1 spoon.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Stirring Until It Vanishes] 1. Half fill the jar from the pitcher and let her tip a spoonful of salt in. It sits on the bottom in a white heap.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Stirring Until It Vanishes] 2. Hand her the spoon. Stirring until the heap disappears takes longer than she expects.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Stirring Until It Vanishes] 3. Ask her where the salt went, then get her to dip a fingertip in this weak jar and taste it. Leave the question open until this afternoon.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Making Sea Water] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Making Sea Water] 1. Tape a label on each jar and write **0**, **1** and **6** with the marker. She fills all three from the pitcher to the same height.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Making Sea Water] 2. Jar 0 stays plain. One spoonful of salt into jar 1. Six into jar 6, stirred until the water goes clear again.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Making Sea Water] 3. Lower the egg into jar 0. It sits flat on the bottom. Try jar 1, where it sits on the bottom again. She met this egg and this jar in March, so let her tell you what happens next.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Making Sea Water] 4. Jar 6. The egg lifts off the bottom and rides at the top with a coin of shell showing, and she will ask you to do it again.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Making Sea Water] 5. Let her move the egg between the jars as long as she wants.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Making Sea Water] 6. Pour a splash from jar 6 onto the dark plate and stand it in full sun. Come back at snack time to a white crust of salt.

    [🌳 Get Outside: Painting the Fence With Water] and the water vanishes behind her, which on a hot morning takes four minutes.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Painting the Fence With Water] Race the drying: a big letter A at one end, then a run back to see whether it
    [🌳 Get Outside: Painting the Fence With Water] survived.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Painting the Fence With Water] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Deep, Dark, Cold Room] The coolest room in the house becomes the bottom of the sea for the afternoon.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Deep, Dark, Cold Room] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Deep, Dark, Cold Room] 1. Shut the curtains and turn the lights off. Bring the fish, the whale and the jellyfish in and hang or lean them round the walls.

  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** A clothesline sits at her neck height and a rope between two trees sits lower. The sheet stays on it all week, so peg something bright along any bare stretch she runs past.     [🎨 Second Main Event: The Paper Campfire] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Paper Campfire] 1. Lay the stones in a ring the size of a bike wheel, a long stride out from the tent door. She places every one.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Paper Campfire] 2. Stand three sticks against each other like a tripod and lean the rest around them. It falls over. Build it again and let her work out that the first three hold the rest up.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Paper Campfire] 3. Tear the paper into flames a hand long, twenty or so, and twist the bottom of each one into a stalk.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Paper Campfire] 4. Tape the stalks low on the sticks so the flames stand upright between them, red near the ground and yellow at the top. Tape paper to stick, never paper to paper.

    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Paper Campfire] 6. Switch it on and kneel in the doorway to look at the fire from inside, where the shade lets the light show.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Paper Campfire] 7. Leave it built. At quarter past four, lift the flashlight out of the tin and take her into the tent with it, then untie the two corner strings so both doorways fall shut. A sheet with the doors down is as dark as July gets.
D212 (Sat Jul 31) The Camp Kitchen
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The oven is on and the parcels come out hot and full of steam, which scalds worse than the tray does.     [🎨 Second Main Event: Striking Camp] 3. Pegs off the line into the peg bag, six at a time so she can count them, then the sheet down and shaken.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Striking Camp] 4. Bedrolls stay tied and go into the baskets with the rug on top.

D218 (Fri Aug 6) The Bounce Test
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Ping-pong balls and anything smaller than a tennis ball fit down Kreston's throat, and at 14 months he walks into the middle of things to get at them. Count the small ones into the basket at the end and stand it up high. The scissors stay with you.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rounding Up the Balls] **🧰 You need:** 1 laundry basket · every ball in the house · 1 orange · 1 pair of clean socks · 1 sheet of kitchen foil.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rounding Up the Balls] 1. Hand her the basket and send her round the house. Under the beds, the closet, the shed shelf. Eight is enough, twelve is better.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rounding Up the Balls] 2. Add three things that are not balls. Roll the socks into one, scrunch the foil into another, put the orange on top.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rounding Up the Balls] 3. Line them up in the order she thinks they bounce, best to worst. You test that order at quarter past nine.
    [🎨 The Main Event: High Bouncer, Low Bouncer] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: High Bouncer, Low Bouncer] 1. Tape a strip to the wall at your shoulder height and write **DROP HERE** on it. Every ball starts against that tape and gets let go, never thrown.
    [🎨 The Main Event: High Bouncer, Low Bouncer] 2. Stand the two baskets a stride back on either side. Tape a label on each, **HIGH** and **LOW**.
    [🎨 The Main Event: High Bouncer, Low Bouncer] 3. Drop the first ball. Watch where the top of the bounce reaches on her, knee or waist or shoulder. She calls it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: High Bouncer, Low Bouncer] 4. Before each new ball she squeezes it and says which basket it lands in. Write the prediction down and tick it after the drop.
    [🎨 The Main Event: High Bouncer, Low Bouncer] 5. Work through the lot on hard floor. The orange divides opinion and the foil ball disappoints her.
    [🎨 The Main Event: High Bouncer, Low Bouncer] 6. Count each basket and read the prediction sheet back, ticks and all. Ask what the high ones have in common and write her answer word for word.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Where the Ball Bounces Best] Take the tennis ball and a piece of chalk to the front and bounce it on
    [🌳 Get Outside: Where the Ball Bounces Best] everything hard enough to bounce on. The paving, the drain cover, the road, the
    [🌳 Get Outside: Where the Ball Bounces Best] grass baked flat since June. Chalk an **H** or an **L** on each as she calls
    [🌳 Get Outside: Where the Ball Bounces Best] it, and finish on the shaded lawn, which still has give in it.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Where the Ball Bounces Best] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Ball Made of Bags and String] Children in half the world make their own footballs, and this is how.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Ball Made of Bags and String] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Ball Made of Bags and String] 1. Scrunch two sheets of newspaper into the tightest ball she can manage. Squeeze, do not fold. That lump sets the size of everything after it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Ball Made of Bags and String] 2. Push the core into the old sock, knot it tight above, and cut the tail off with your scissors.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Ball Made of Bags and String] 3. Wrap a grocery bag round the sock and twist the handles down flat. Then the next over the twist from a different side, four to six in all.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Ball Made of Bags and String] 4. Hold the bundle while she winds the string. Round, then across, then round again. Twenty turns leaves an egg. Fifty looks like a ball.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Ball Made of Bags and String] 5. Tie the string off, cover the knot with packing tape, and run masking tape over any bag still showing.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Ball Made of Bags and String] 6. Take it to the taped mark on the wall. She calls high or low, then drops it, and finds she was right about a sock ball.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Ball Made of Bags and String] 7. Out on the grass, kick it. It flies heavy and dead and will not roll far, which is what makes it good on a dry lawn.
D219 (Sat Aug 7) Team Colors
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Use a plastic bottle for the shake rather than a glass jar, or keep your hand round the jar while she shakes it. Washable non-toxic paint only, because a 14-month-old gets a hand in it. Pegs go back on the line at the end rather than down on the paving.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Oil, Water and a Good Shake] **🧰 You need:** 1 clear jar with a lid · water · 2 tablespoons cooking oil · 1 tablespoon · a drop of food coloring or watered paint.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Oil, Water and a Good Shake] 1. Half fill the jar with water and let her stir a drop of color through it.

    [🌅 Opening Activity: Oil, Water and a Good Shake] 3. Stand it on the table and watch. The oil climbs back to the top every time. Tell her a crayon does the same trick at quarter past nine.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Number the Paint Runs Off] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Number the Paint Runs Off] 1. She picks the team's two colors out of the jars and names the team. Write the name on the back of one sheet in marker.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Number the Paint Runs Off] 2. Give her the white crayon and a sheet of thick paper. She draws a big number, pressing hard. It comes out close to invisible.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Number the Paint Runs Off] 3. Load the wide brush with the first watery color and wash it edge to edge in one direction. The number rises up through it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Number the Paint Runs Off] 4. Rinse the brush in the jar of clean water and dry it on the towel before the second color, or you get mud.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Number the Paint Runs Off] 5. Do a second sheet in the other color, and a third with a stripe of each meeting in the middle.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Number the Paint Runs Off] 6. Hand her the last three sheets to do on her own. Write a squad number on the back of each and lay them out of the sun to dry.

    [🌳 Get Outside: Ten Kicks Against the Shed Wall] the wall still has shade. Ten kicks each, counted out loud, and a chalk tally on
    [🌳 Get Outside: Ten Kicks Against the Shed Wall] the paving for every one that lands inside the box. Leave the tally where it is.

    [🎨 Second Main Event: Numbers on Real Shirts] The wax trick works on cloth too, and this one she wears to the museum.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Numbers on Real Shirts] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Numbers on Real Shirts] 1. Slide a piece of cardboard inside each shirt, up to the shoulders. Paint goes through cotton in seconds and the card is the only thing stopping it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Numbers on Real Shirts] 2. Tear the masking tape into strips and build a number on the back of the first shirt. Straight lines only. A 1 is one strip, a 7 is two, a 4 is three. Press every edge down hard.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Numbers on Real Shirts] 3. Let her build the next number herself. Crooked is correct. If she wants a 12, both digits go on.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Numbers on Real Shirts] 4. Load the sponge and press it straight down over the tape rather than brushing across. Brushing lifts the edges and the paint creeps underneath.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Numbers on Real Shirts] 5. Cover the whole back panel, tape and all, and stop while the paint is wet.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Numbers on Real Shirts] 6. Peel the tape off there and then, corner first, pulling it back on itself. The number underneath stays white.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Numbers on Real Shirts] 7. Clip the shirts on the line in the sun. August dries a t-shirt inside an hour.
D220 (Sun Aug 8) Somebody Has to Say Go
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** A whistle is the exact size Kreston chokes on, and the pea inside it comes loose. It goes round your neck or in a high drawer, never on the floor. Keep him out of the running lane. At 14 months he walks straight into the path of a sprinting three-year-old.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: One Blast, Two Blasts, Three] **🧰 You need:** 1 whistle · 2 cushions · the hallway.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: One Blast, Two Blasts, Three] 1. Sit her at the table with the whistle between you. Blow it once. Then twice. Then three times.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: One Blast, Two Blasts, Three] 2. Set the meanings out loud. One blast stop, two turn round, three everybody down on the floor. Have her say them back in order.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: One Blast, Two Blasts, Three] 3. Practice sitting down. One blast and she freezes in her chair, three and she slides onto the floor. Then drop a cushion at each end of the hall.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Statues on the Whistle] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Statues on the Whistle] 1. Tape a start line and a finish line at each end of the hallway and stand a cushion past each. Those are the turns.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Statues on the Whistle] 2. Music on, and send her line to line however she wants to travel. Running, crawling, sideways, hopping.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Statues on the Whistle] 3. One blast. She holds whatever shape she is in, mid-stride, until the next blast. Count three out loud while she wobbles, then let her go.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Statues on the Whistle] 4. Add the second signal. Two blasts, she turns at the nearest cushion and comes back. Give her four or five before the third arrives.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Statues on the Whistle] 5. Three blasts, everybody on the floor including you. Getting down there yourself is what makes her drop fast.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Statues on the Whistle] 6. Hand her the whistle for the last ten minutes and run when she says run. She holds the stop for an unreasonable length of time.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stop and Go Through the Sprinkler] Set the sprinkler on the grass and run the same three signals through it. One
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stop and Go Through the Sprinkler] blast and she freezes in the spray. Two and she goes back through it the other
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stop and Go Through the Sprinkler] way. Get out before eleven while the grass is cool underfoot, and take the
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stop and Go Through the Sprinkler] whistle with you.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stop and Go Through the Sprinkler] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Peg Scoreboard] The rest of the week needs somewhere to put its points. It gets built once,
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Peg Scoreboard] sitting down, and used on three more days.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Peg Scoreboard] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Peg Scoreboard] 1. Lay the card flat and rule a line down the middle. Two teams. She names both and writes what she can of the names at the top.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Peg Scoreboard] 2. Tape one string across the upper half, the other across the lower half. Pull each tight before the second tape goes down, or the clothespins sag instead of sliding.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Peg Scoreboard] 3. Mark ten spaces under each string. Nought at the left, then a line for every point up to ten. She does the marks under the second string.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Peg Scoreboard] 4. Clip a peg to the left end of each string. That is nought all, and a point moves a peg one space right.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Peg Scoreboard] 5. Stick a sticker at the five and the ten on both strings, or tape a bottle cap there, so she finds halfway without counting.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Peg Scoreboard] 6. Test it. Three points to you, four to her, one space at a time and counting out loud, then slide both back.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Peg Scoreboard] 7. Stand it against the wall where it lives all week, spare pegs clipped along the top edge.

  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Use washed play sand, not builder's sand.   SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The bell is choke-sized and it travels hand to hand round a circle Kreston is sitting in. Count it in, count it out, and keep it in your hand between rounds.     [🎨 The Main Event: The Sound That Goes Round] 2. Start with a clap. You clap once, the person on your left claps once, and it goes round until it comes back to you. Three times.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Sound That Goes Round] 3. Add the shaker. One shake each, passed hand to hand, and nobody shakes it out of turn. She will police this harder than you do.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Sound That Goes Round] 4. Pass the beanbag round instead. Whoever holds it makes any sound at all, and the next person copies it before passing it on.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Sound That Goes Round] 5. Put the bell in. The bell ends the round, so whoever it reaches rings it once and everybody stops dead. Start it from a different place each time.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Sound That Goes Round] 6. Give her the beanbag last and let her invent the sound everybody copies. Get it wrong on purpose once and let her correct you.

    [🌳 Get Outside: The Circle of Found Things] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

    [🎨 Second Main Event: Whoever Holds the Spoon Sings] 1. Spread the blanket flat in the middle of the circle. The spoon lives there between turns, a place to put it down that is nobody's.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Whoever Holds the Spoon Sings] 2. Set the rule out loud once: whoever holds the spoon chooses the song, everybody sings it with them, and nobody starts a song while the spoon is on the blanket.

    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Letter Window] 1. Show her what it does first. Lay the card flat on a page and slide it until one letter fills the window. Say that letter's sound.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Letter Window] 2. Hand the card over. She slides it wherever she likes and you say the sound of whatever lands in the frame.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Letter Window] 3. Now hunt. Pick her A and go looking. She slides the window along a line and stops when she thinks she has one.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Letter Window] 4. Every A she finds earns a mark on the notepad, one straight line in marker, her hand and not yours.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Letter Window] 5. Any page with a lot of them gets a torn scrap laid in. She comes back to those.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Letter Window] 6. Change the letter. Save the Z until last, because she will hunt a whole book before it turns up.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Letter Window] 7. Count the marks together at the end and say each sound out loud as she counts.
D273 (Thu Sep 30) Letters You Can Eat
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The oven is on through the middle of the morning and Kreston runs at 15 months. Say it out loud each time either comes near, keep him out of the kitchen while the tray moves, and cool the tray on a high surface rather than the table. Tip the bucket out the moment she stops painting the fence. A bucket of water at Kreston's height is a drowning risk even at two inches. Raw dough is not for eating, by anyone. Keep the cut string on the table and the ball in the drawer; loose cord at his height is a strangulation risk.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rubbing In the Butter] **🧰 You need:** 250 g all-purpose flour · 75 g superfine sugar · 175 g softened butter · 1 mixing bowl · 1 plate for the fridge.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rubbing In the Butter] 1. Flour and sugar into the bowl. She stirs it with a whole hand rather than a spoon.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rubbing In the Butter] 2. Butter in, torn into lumps. Rub it through your fingers together until the bowl looks like damp sand, a good ten minutes.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rubbing In the Butter] 3. Squeeze the lot into one ball, onto the plate and into the fridge until 9:15. Cold dough holds a letter; warm dough spreads.
    [🎨 The Main Event: A Cookie for Every Initial] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: A Cookie for Every Initial] 1. The ball from the fridge is 250 g all-purpose flour, 75 g superfine sugar and 175 g softened butter, rubbed together and chilled. Oven on at 160°C, tray lined with baking paper.
    [🎨 The Main Event: A Cookie for Every Initial] 2. Flour the board. Cut the dough into four with the table knife and hand her the first piece. She rolls it under both flat hands into a rope as thick as her finger; a stubborn lump gets the rolling pin.
    [🎨 The Main Event: A Cookie for Every Initial] 3. Bend the rope into a letter on the tray. **B** for Brooklyn, **J** for Joseph, **A** for Azlyn, **K** for Kreston. Say whose it is as it goes down.
    [🎨 The Main Event: A Cookie for Every Initial] 4. Trim the overshooting ends with the table knife and press every join hard, or the letter comes out of the oven in pieces. Lay the trimmings in a short line of their own on the tray and bake them with the letters; that line is Kreston's, and it saves the four.
    [🎨 The Main Event: A Cookie for Every Initial] 5. Fifteen to eighteen minutes, until pale gold and no darker. Slide the paper onto the wire rack and leave them until stone cold; a warm letter breaks in her hand.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Water Letters on the Fence] Fill a bucket, take the widest brush in the house out to the fence and let her
    [🌳 Get Outside: Water Letters on the Fence] paint letters two feet tall on the panels. They come up dark, hold a minute and
    [🌳 Get Outside: Water Letters on the Fence] fade from the top down as the wood dries, so she works fast and starts again.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Water Letters on the Fence] Late September wood is damp, so hers last.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Water Letters on the Fence] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Three Parcels That Have to Last] Four cookies came out of the oven and one is hers. The other three have to
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Three Parcels That Have to Last] survive on a shelf until six.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Three Parcels That Have to Last] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Three Parcels That Have to Last] 1. Lay the four cookies out and let her eat hers in front of the other three. Those three are for tonight and she is in charge of them.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Three Parcels That Have to Last] 2. One sheet of paper per parcel and one spare. She covers them in crayon while you say whose each is. Write the name big across the top in permanent marker and she copies the first letter underneath.

D276 (Sun Oct 3) Counting With Her Ears
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Coins, pebbles, dried pasta and buttons are every one of them the size that chokes a 15-month-old, and Kreston runs. Sweep the floor between rounds, count the coins back into the tin before he goes down, and the counting jar goes up high the moment the round ends.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: One at a Time Into the Tin] **🧰 You need:** 1 empty metal cookie tin · 10 coins or smooth pebbles.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: One at a Time Into the Tin] 1. Drop one coin into the empty tin from a hand's height. Then two, one after the other, a beat apart, and say the number each time it lands.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: One at a Time Into the Tin] 2. Hand her the coins. She drops, you count out loud, and the two of you find the speed where the numbers still fit between the sounds.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: One at a Time Into the Tin] 3. Tip the tin out and count them back in together. Tell her that at 9:15 she does the same job with her eyes shut.
    [🎨 The Main Event: How Many Did You Hear] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: How Many Did You Hear] 1. Sit facing each other across the table with the tin between you and the dish towel draped over your dropping hand.
    [🎨 The Main Event: How Many Did You Hear] 2. She shuts her eyes. Drop between one and five coins, one at a time with a clear gap, and let her say the number before she opens them.
    [🎨 The Main Event: How Many Did You Hear] 3. Count them out of the tin together to check. She will be right on two and three long before she is right on five.
    [🎨 The Main Event: How Many Did You Hear] 4. Swap over. She drops, you guess, and you get one wrong on purpose to see what she does with that.
    [🎨 The Main Event: How Many Did You Hear] 5. Change the sound. Dried pasta lands soft, a pebble lands hard, and the wooden spoon on the tin rim is louder than either. Ask her which is easiest to count and take her answer.
    [🎨 The Main Event: How Many Did You Hear] 6. Refill from the counting jar when the coins run short, and put the jar back up high when you finish.
    [🌳 Get Outside: One Finger for Every Sound] Walk to a bench, sit down and shut your eyes for a slow count of ten. One
    [🌳 Get Outside: One Finger for Every Sound] finger goes up for every separate sound: a car, a dog, a plane, feet on wet
    [🌳 Get Outside: One Finger for Every Sound] leaves. Compare hands at the end. October Sunday mornings are quiet enough for
    [🌳 Get Outside: One Finger for Every Sound] this to work and cold enough that you both want to move again after.
    [🌳 Get Outside: One Finger for Every Sound] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Knock Three Times, Bring Me Three] A number she cannot see has to be held in her head long enough to act on it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Knock Three Times, Bring Me Three] The knocking is the order and the basket is the answer.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Knock Three Times, Bring Me Three] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Knock Three Times, Bring Me Three] 1. Knock three times on the table, slow. She fetches three things from the room and puts them in the basket, whatever they turn out to be.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Knock Three Times, Bring Me Three] 2. Count them out together. Draw three marks on the paper with the crayon and write **3** at the end of the row.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Knock Three Times, Bring Me Three] 3. Go up and down between one and five. Empty the basket after each round so she starts from nothing.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Knock Three Times, Bring Me Three] 4. Move out of sight and knock on the door instead. She has to count with nothing to look at, which is the version she finds hard.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Knock Three Times, Bring Me Three] 5. Swap over. She knocks with the wooden spoon and sends you off, and you come back with the wrong number twice so she gets to correct you.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Knock Three Times, Bring Me Three] 6. Counting jar round: she counts that many buttons out of the jar into the basket, then tips them back and the jar goes up high.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Knock Three Times, Bring Me Three] 7. Finish on a big one. Knock eight times and watch her carry what she can and come back for the rest.
D277 (Mon Oct 4) Loud Numbers
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Close-range banging is loud enough to damage a 15-month-old's hearing. Keep Kreston a room's width from the buckets, work in short bursts with quiet gaps, and stop the moment he startles, covers his ears or turns away. Never let the spoons swing near his head.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Five Buckets, Biggest to Smallest] **🧰 You need:** 5 buckets, tins or saucepans in different sizes · 1 wooden spoon.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Five Buckets, Biggest to Smallest] 1. Line the five up across the floor, biggest to smallest, and let her move any she disagrees with.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Five Buckets, Biggest to Smallest] 2. Tap each one twice with the spoon, working down the line. The big ones boom and the small ones ring, and she will hear the order change.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Five Buckets, Biggest to Smallest] 3. Ask her which one is the loudest and let her prove it. That one goes on the end and it is number five at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Five Is the Loudest] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Five Is the Loudest] 1. Put Kreston on his cushion at the far end of the room, a good distance back, before anything gets hit.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Five Is the Loudest] 2. Stick a strip of masking tape on each bucket and number them 1 to 5 in marker, quietest to loudest, in the order she settled on at breakfast.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Five Is the Loudest] 3. Fold the card into five panels and write a big numeral on each. That is the dial, and she holds it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Five Is the Loudest] 4. She shows a number and everyone plays eight beats at that volume. Count the eight out loud together, spoons on the buckets, and stop dead on eight.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Five Is the Loudest] 5. She changes the number between rounds. One is a fingertip on the rim, five is both spoons on the big bucket, and she calls which arrives.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Five Is the Loudest] 6. Count down to finish. Five, four, three, two, one, then zero, and zero is silence held for a slow count of five with the spoons on the floor.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Loud Twenty, Quiet Twenty] Find a path with nobody's window on it and count twenty steps at the top of
    [🌳 Get Outside: Loud Twenty, Quiet Twenty] your voices, then twenty more in a whisper. October wind takes the loud numbers
    [🌳 Get Outside: Loud Twenty, Quiet Twenty] off across the field, and the quiet ones make her lean in to hear yours.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Loud Twenty, Quiet Twenty] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Zero to Ten Down the Hallway] The hall floor becomes a number line this afternoon, and it stays down until
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Zero to Ten Down the Hallway] Thursday.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Zero to Ten Down the Hallway] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Zero to Ten Down the Hallway] 1. Write 0 to 10 on the eleven sheets in marker, one numeral per sheet, and let her do the ones she can.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Zero to Ten Down the Hallway] 2. Tape them down the hall, a stride apart, 0 at the near end and 10 at the far one. She lays them, you tape.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Zero to Ten Down the Hallway] 3. Walk the line together saying each number as a foot lands on it. Then back the other way, 10 down to 0.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Zero to Ten Down the Hallway] 4. Call a number and she runs to it. Call another before she has stopped. Six or seven rounds and she stops looking at 0 to work out where 7 is.

D279 (Wed Oct 6) Stop on Seven
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Buttons are the size that chokes a 16-month-old. The jar round happens at the table with Kreston out of reach, the buttons are counted back in before he is on the rug again, and the jar goes up high after. Use tins for the tower only if the rims are smooth.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Number That Means Freeze] **🧰 You need:** 11 blank cards · 1 thick marker · masking tape.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Number That Means Freeze] 1. Write 0 to 10 on the cards, one numeral each, and let her take over on the ones she knows. These are the number cards for the rest of the week.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Number That Means Freeze] 2. Spread all eleven face up on the table and ask her to choose the one that means stop. She will pick a big one.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Number That Means Freeze] 3. Tape her choice to the door at her eye height. That number is the rule now, and everyone in the house has to obey it at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Number on the Door] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Number on the Door] 1. Read the card on the door out loud together. Say it back and forth until she can tell you what happens when the count reaches it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Number on the Door] 2. Bang the pan with the spoon and count up from one, slow and even, while she runs the hall from 0 to 10 and back.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Number on the Door] 3. On her number she freezes. Keep banging past it. The count is the cue, so the noise carrying on is what makes the number do the work.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Number on the Door] 4. Praise the stop, not the running. Hold the freeze for a slow three before you start the next count.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Number on the Door] 5. New card every round. She takes one off the table and tapes it up herself, and low numbers come round fast.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Number on the Door] 6. Swap over. She counts and bangs, you run the hall, and you miss the stop once so she gets to call you out.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stopping at Every Third Tree] Walk the road counting trees or lampposts out loud, and stop dead at every
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stopping at Every Third Tree] third one. Both of you, hands in the air, until she says go. October has the
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stopping at Every Third Tree] sidewalk covered, so count the piles she can kick through on the way as well.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stopping at Every Third Tree] Turn round at the fifteenth and count them back down to one.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stopping at Every Third Tree] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tower That Stops Where She Says] She sits down for this one, and the rule comes with her. Stopping a hand mid-air
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tower That Stops Where She Says] is harder than stopping two feet.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tower That Stops Where She Says] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tower That Stops Where She Says] 1. Line the ten cups up beside her and put Kreston's four blocks down at the far end of the rug. She picks a card and props it against the pile.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tower That Stops Where She Says] 2. She stacks one cup at a time, counting each one, and stops dead on her number with the next cup already in her hand.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tower That Stops Where She Says] 3. Hold it there. Count a slow three with the cup in the air, then let her carry on to ten.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tower That Stops Where She Says] 4. Bring the counting jar down to the table for one round. She drops buttons into a cup one at a time and stops on her number, then tips them back and the jar goes up high again.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tower That Stops Where She Says] 5. Paper and crayons out. She makes her own stop cards, a numeral and a picture on each, and they can be any numbers she likes.

D280 (Thu Oct 7) Clap It Back
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Hang the string high enough that neither child can reach it, tied at both ends and never left in a loop. Take it down and put it away the moment the cards come off, because a length of string at toddler height is a strangulation risk. Buttons go on the floor only while the two of you are sitting over them, and they are back in the jar and up high before Kreston is down.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Two Claps, Then Wait] **🧰 You need:** your hands · her hands · a floor to sit on.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Two Claps, Then Wait] 1. Sit facing each other and clap twice. Wait. She claps twice back, and neither of you says anything about what the game is.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Two Claps, Then Wait] 2. Three claps, then four. Count them out loud as you go so the number arrives with the sound.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Two Claps, Then Wait] 3. Four claps with a gap in the middle. She will send back four in a row and lose the gap.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Beats, Then Your Turn] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Beats, Then Your Turn] 1. Sit on the floor with the pan and the buckets between you and a spoon each.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Beats, Then Your Turn] 2. Play four even beats on the pan, counting them out loud. She plays four back. Do it until it bores her, which takes about six goes.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Beats, Then Your Turn] 3. Take the counting out. Play four beats in silence and let her count them in her head before she answers.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Beats, Then Your Turn] 4. Lay one button from the counting jar on the floor for each beat as you play it, and leave a space where a beat is missing. She reads the row and plays it. The jar goes back up high when this round ends.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Beats, Then Your Turn] 5. Move the same four beats around: hands, knees, floor, pan, bucket. The pattern stays the same and the sound changes.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Beats, Then Your Turn] 6. She leads now. She plays, you copy, and you get one wrong on purpose so she has to fix you.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Railings and the Stick] Find a stretch of railings and run a stick along them, counting the clicks.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Railings and the Stick] Slow first, then fast enough that neither of you can keep up with the number.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Railings and the Stick] Wet October railings ring where dry ones clack, and the same stick on a
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Railings and the Stick] wooden fence goes dull. Try both and let her tell you which one she likes.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Railings and the Stick] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Four Dots and a Gap] A rhythm disappears the second it stops. This afternoon hers goes onto a card
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Four Dots and a Gap] and stays there.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Four Dots and a Gap] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Four Dots and a Gap] 1. She plays four beats. Draw them on a card in marker as she plays, a dot for a beat and a gap for a silence.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Four Dots and a Gap] 2. Hand her the card and ask her to play what is on it. Point at each dot as she hits it so her eye travels along the row.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Four Dots and a Gap] 3. Six cards, six patterns, three hers and three yours, with a gap at the front of one.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Four Dots and a Gap] 4. She colors the cards with crayons. Every dot she colors is one she has to look at.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Four Dots and a Gap] 5. Tie the string tight to two door handles, high, one knot at each end. She pegs the cards along it in the order she picks, a peg to a card.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Four Dots and a Gap] 6. Play the whole row left to right, spoons on the buckets, counting the dots on each card as you reach it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Four Dots and a Gap] 7. Finish the week: tap Tuesday's jars from one to eight with the metal teaspoon, walk the hall number line from 0 to 10, then play the card row once more.   SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The craft knife is adult-only and goes back onto a high shelf between cuts, not onto the arm of the sofa.   SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Cardboard taped over stair treads is a slip hazard for everybody in the house. It comes up the moment you stop racing, and nobody uses those stairs while it is down.   SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The rope stays in your hands or in the drawer, never left in a loop on the floor. D289 (Sat Oct 16) Moon Dust and Craters
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Marbles and small stones are the right size to choke a 16-month-old, and they roll out of the tray. Count them in and out, work on the sheet, and sweep the floor before Kreston goes down anywhere near it. Loose flour travels, so keep it off the floor he crawls and walks on.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Sifting the Moon Dust] **🧰 You need:** 1 old sheet or towel · 1 deep roasting tin · 1 kg all-purpose flour · 2 tbsp cocoa powder · 1 sieve.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Sifting the Moon Dust] 1. Spread the sheet on the kitchen floor and stand the tin in the middle. Everything happens on the sheet today.

    [🌅 Opening Activity: Sifting the Moon Dust] 3. Sift the cocoa over the top so the whole tray goes gray. Leave it untouched until 9:15 and tell her nothing about what comes next.

    [🎨 The Main Event: The Craters She Makes Herself] 4. Fish every stone and marble out with the spoon, counting them into the cup.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Craters She Makes Herself] 5. Rebuild: sieve spare flour over the tray, smooth it, dust the cocoa back on. A minute's work and she has a clean moon.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Craters She Makes Herself] 6. Then let her drop everything she is allowed to drop, in any order, until the tray is holes on holes.

D310 (Sat Nov 6) The Cardboard Cow
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The pin is yours. Make every hole yourself and count it back into your hand at the end. Warm water, never hot, tested on your wrist. A bucket with water in it is a drowning risk for a 17-month-old, so it never sits on the floor unattended and it gets emptied when the milking stops. Keep the second towel down; the wet floor is a slip risk for everyone.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Four Fingers and a Pin] **🧰 You need:** 1 dishwashing glove · 1 straight pin (**adult only**) · warm water · the sink.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Four Fingers and a Pin] 1. Fill the glove at the tap, hold the wrist shut in your fist and hand it to her two-handed. It has fingers and it is heavy.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Four Fingers and a Pin] 2. Hold it over the sink and prick one fingertip with the pin. Her hands stay behind her back. One hole, one thin jet.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Four Fingers and a Pin] 3. Prick the other three and let her squeeze them out. The cow arrives at 9:15. Put the pin out of reach.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Milking the Glove] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Milking the Glove] 1. Towels down first, one under the working spot and one folded by the door.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Milking the Glove] 2. Build the cow. Box on its side, flaps taped shut with packing tape, a face on one end in marker. She picks the head end.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Milking the Glove] 3. Fill a glove with warm water, tested on the inside of your wrist. Twist the wrist end over and tape it shut hard.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Milking the Glove] 4. Prick four fingertips once each with the pin over the sink, because a cow has four teats. Leave the thumb. Tape the glove under the box so all four fingers hang at her sitting height.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Milking the Glove] 5. Stool under the cow, bucket under the glove, her on the stool. Show her the grip once: thumb and finger closed at the top, then squeeze down. Pulling gets nothing.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Milking the Glove] 6. Refill and run it again. Three gloves is a morning's milking, and the last one can have eight holes.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Boot Prints in the Mud] Find the softest ground within reach, at a field gate or the edge of a path

    [🌳 Get Outside: Boot Prints in the Mud] straight up for a clean print. Put yours beside hers. Then hunt the same
    [🌳 Get Outside: Boot Prints in the Mud] stretch for prints neither of you made: dog, bird, hoof.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Boot Prints in the Mud] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tray You Can Rub Out] The salt tray is the calmest thing in this book. Clear the table and set it up
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tray You Can Rub Out] while she watches.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tray You Can Rub Out] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tray You Can Rub Out] 1. Line the tray with the dark card, or foil shiny side up, so her lines show black.

  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Boots slide on bare boards with the rug up, so check the floor is dry before the music goes on, and nobody dances in socks.     [🌳 Get Outside: Splashing Down the Muddy Lane] stood in. Run the rhyme game as you walk, one pair per puddle, and let her

D316 (Fri Nov 12) Catching the Rain
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Cut bottle edges are sharp, so tape every rim before either child is near them, and the heavy scissors stay with you.     [🌅 Opening Activity: Marking Up the Bottles] 2. Now the real scale. Hold each bottle steady while she copies her lines up the side of it with the permanent marker, bottom to top. Paper and tape come off a wet bottle inside a week, and marker stays on until spring.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Marking Up the Bottles] 3. Tell her the bottles go outside to catch rain and ask where they should stand. Leave her thinking while you clear the table.

    [🎨 The Main Event: Where the Rain Lands] 2. Let her drop a handful of stones into each one. Wind takes an empty bottle off a step in seconds.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Where the Rain Lands] 3. Walk her round outside and let her pick three spots: open ground, under something, tight against a wall. Write OPEN, UNDER and WALL on them with the marker.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Where the Rain Lands] 4. Ask which will catch the most. Write her answer on masking tape and stick it to the back door.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Where the Rain Lands] 5. If the sky stays dry, hand her the watering can and let her rain on all three from the same height, counting to five over each.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Where the Rain Lands] 6. Read them at 2:30 and again before dinner, down at her level, counting the lines up each scale out loud.
