    [🌳 Get Outside: The Puddle Round] let her test each one with a stick before she commits her boot. The deep one by
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Puddle Round] the drain is the prize. Come back past the bottles to see whether anything has
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Puddle Round] landed in them.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Puddle Round] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tube That Sounds Like Rain] An hour of building buys a tube that sounds like rain on the window.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tube That Sounds Like Rain] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tube That Sounds Like Rain] 1. Squash the foil into a long loose rope, twist it, and feed it down the tube. It has to rub the sides all the way down.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tube That Sounds Like Rain] 2. Draw round the tube end on the thick card twice, then cut both circles a finger wider than the line. You cut, she holds the card.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tube That Sounds Like Rain] 3. Tape one circle over the bottom end with packing tape, twice round and once across.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tube That Sounds Like Rain] 4. She pours the rice in a handful at a time. One cup and no more, then tape the second circle on the same way.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tube That Sounds Like Rain] 5. Wrap the paper round the tube and let her cover it with crayon. Hold it on with masking tape at both ends.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tube That Sounds Like Rain] 6. Tip it end over end. Slow, and it is a shower. Fast, and the whole storm arrives at once.

  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Hot liquid scalds, so warm the milk, let it stand, and test a cup against your own wrist before she carries anything.     [🌳 Get Outside: Breath You Can See] ones and short fast ones look different. Find frost at the bottom of the north
    [🌳 Get Outside: Breath You Can See] wall for a bare fingertip.

D321 (Wed Nov 17) Thunder and Lightning
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** A baking tray banged at close range is loud enough to hurt Kreston's ears, so keep him the length of the room from the thunder and stop if he startles. Make the rice tub one he cannot open, and check the lid after every take. Loose rice is a choking hazard at 17 months.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Flash, Then Bang] **🧰 You need:** 1 flashlight · 1 baking tray · 1 wooden spoon · the hallway with the door shut.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Flash, Then Bang] 1. Curtains drawn. You flash the flashlight once, and she counts out loud until you tap the tray with the spoon.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Flash, Then Bang] 2. Swap over. She flashes, you count, she bangs.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Flash, Then Bang] 3. Stretch the gap: one count, then five, then ten. A long count means the storm is far away.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Storm She Conducts] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Storm She Conducts] 1. A cushion down for each of you. She stands at one end with Kreston beside her, and you sit at the far end with the tray.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Storm She Conducts] 2. Build the sounds one at a time and name each: fingertips on the floor is the first rain, the bedsheet shaken is wind, the rain tube tipped is the downpour, the flashlight is lightning, the tray and spoon are thunder.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Storm She Conducts] 3. Give her the shape and nothing else. A storm starts small, builds, and stops. She calls each sound and whoever has it does it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Storm She Conducts] 4. Run it once with her conducting. She will go to the thunder inside ten seconds, so let that storm be short and start again.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Storm She Conducts] 5. Run it again and ask for the quiet parts. Rain on its own, then rain and wind, and hold the tray back until she calls it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Storm She Conducts] 6. Finish on her word for the end. Everybody stops, she opens the curtains, the storm has passed.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Rain on the Umbrella] Take one big umbrella out into whatever November is doing and stand under it at
    [🌳 Get Outside: Rain on the Umbrella] the end of the road. Say nothing and listen. Then move under a tree, out into
    [🌳 Get Outside: Rain on the Umbrella] the open, then under a porch. The same rain sounds different on every roof. Let
    [🌳 Get Outside: Rain on the Umbrella] her hold the umbrella, though it will end up over the sidewalk.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Rain on the Umbrella] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Raw egg. She washes her hands after cracking and nobody licks the bowl, Kreston included. Hot fat spits the moment batter hits it, so she ladles from the side with your hand over hers and the pan handle turned in.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Separating the Yolks] **🧰 You need:** 4 eggs · 2 large bowls, one metal or glass · 1 fork.

    [🌅 Opening Activity: Separating the Yolks] 2. The white runs between her fingers and the yolk stays in her palm. It goes into the second bowl. A broken one joins the yolks and you crack another.

    [🎨 The Main Event: Whisking Until It Stands Up] 2. Pinch of salt over the whites. She whisks while you count sixty, then you take sixty, then her again. With no whisk in the house, the whites go in the jam jar with the lid on and she shakes.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Whisking Until It Stands Up] 3. Stop at the loose foam stage. Lift the whisk out and show her the trail it leaves in the surface, then hand it straight back.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Whisking Until It Stands Up] 4. Once the foam turns white and holds a mark, rain the tablespoon of sugar in while somebody keeps whisking.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Whisking Until It Stands Up] 5. Pull the whisk up and look at the peak. A tip that flops needs more. A tip that stands is done, and it takes longer than either of you expects.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Whisking Until It Stands Up] 6. Hold the bowl upside down over her head. Nothing lands on her, and she will ask for that eleven times.

    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shape Left in the Dough] 3. Cut ten squares with the table knife, your hand over hers. Rough edges are fine.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shape Left in the Dough] 4. One object per square, pressed straight down, held for a count of three, lifted straight up. A sideways lift smears the print.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shape Left in the Dough] 5. Pencil on the back of each: her initial, and a number matching its place in the line.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shape Left in the Dough] 6. Onto the parchment-lined tray and into the oven at its lowest setting. Two hours does it, so in at 10:00 and out at 12:00. Spread them on a cold tray and leave them until 1:00, because warm tiles crumble.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Prints in the Frost] Morning frost sits on grass, car roofs and every shaded patch the low sun
    [🌳 Get Outside: Prints in the Frost] misses. Walk her across a white lawn and turn round to look at her boot trail.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Prints in the Frost] Find a set that is not hers: a dog, a bird, a bike tire. Press her hand on a
    [🌳 Get Outside: Prints in the Frost] frosted hood and count how long the print lasts.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Prints in the Frost] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Digging Up What She Made] The tiles have gone hard, and now they go under the oats.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Digging Up What She Made] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Digging Up What She Made] 1. Oats into the deep tray three fingers deep, on the kitchen floor and off the carpet.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Digging Up What She Made] 2. She turns her back. Bury the ten tiles at different depths and rake the surface flat.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Digging Up What She Made] 3. Show her once and hand it over: the spoon moves the oats, the brush clears the last of it. Dig wide, brush close.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Digging Up What She Made] 4. Every tile comes out face down. She turns it over, says what made it, then checks against the object.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Digging Up What She Made] 5. Line the tiles along the floor with their objects beside them.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Digging Up What She Made] 6. One index card per tile. She dictates and you write it with the marker, word for word: *the bottom of my shoe*.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Digging Up What She Made] 7. Swap. She buries them for you and stands over the tray telling you where you are wrong.
D363 (Wed Dec 29) Mom's Day Off
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Hard candy, chocolate coins and foil wrappers across the floor are a choking hazard for an 18-month-old, so he spends the sorting hour in the high chair with his bowls, and the floor gets swept before he is down. Lit candles on a cake sit at his eye level and he will reach. Put his chair on the far side and blow them out early. The cake knife is yours, and it goes back in the drawer as soon as the slices are cut.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Everything Sweet in the House] **🧰 You need:** 1 large tray or baking sheet · her · three trips.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Everything Sweet in the House] 1. Sit down on the sofa. That is your first job of the day and it holds until the walk.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Everything Sweet in the House] 2. Send her for every piece of candy in the house. Stockings, the cupboard, the bowl by the door, coat pockets. The top of the fridge is yours, pointed out from the sofa.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Everything Sweet in the House] 3. It all lands on the tray at your feet. She makes three trips and finds things you forgot you hid.
    [🎨 The Main Event: One of Every Kind in a Row] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: One of Every Kind in a Row] 1. Tip the tray out on the floor in front of her. You stay on the sofa the whole hour.
    [🎨 The Main Event: One of Every Kind in a Row] 2. One kind per cup of the muffin tin. Her categories, so everything gold may end up together.
    [🎨 The Main Event: One of Every Kind in a Row] 3. Count each cup out loud with her. She says the number, you write it on the paper with the marker.
    [🎨 The Main Event: One of Every Kind in a Row] 4. She names each group. Write her name for it, tear the paper into labels, tape one to each cup.
    [🎨 The Main Event: One of Every Kind in a Row] 5. Lay one of every kind in a row along the empty tray.
    [🎨 The Main Event: One of Every Kind in a Row] 6. One sandwich bag per person, labeled and taped shut. She deals the shares out and decides what fair means. Kreston's goes on a high shelf, unopened.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Lights Left On in the Daytime] Half the street has left its lights on in daylight this week and they look
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Lights Left On in the Daytime] washed out at eleven in the morning. Walk the block counting lit windows, then
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Lights Left On in the Daytime] count again at 5:00 to see how different the same houses look. It is your
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Lights Left On in the Daytime] birthday, so Azlyn pushes Kreston for a whole block.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Lights Left On in the Daytime] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Birthday Azlyn Runs] Azlyn runs this from the frosting to the singing, and the one adult job is
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Birthday Azlyn Runs] lighting the candles.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Birthday Azlyn Runs] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Birthday Azlyn Runs] 1. Cake onto the plate. She spreads the frosting with her fingers and nobody smooths it after her.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Birthday Azlyn Runs] 2. Sprinkles next, the whole shaker, from whatever height she chooses. What lands on the plate stays on the plate.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Birthday Azlyn Runs] 3. Paper and crayons on the floor for the card. Nobody tells her what to draw.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Birthday Azlyn Runs] 4. The present: the thing from the house, wrapped in newspaper, taped by her, ribbon tied by you because a bow beats a three-year-old.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Birthday Azlyn Runs] 5. Candles in. She spaces them and counts them. You strike the matches, the only part she does not do.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Birthday Azlyn Runs] 6. She calls everyone to the table and says where each person sits, including you, on your own birthday.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Birthday Azlyn Runs] 7. Singing, blowing out, and the first slice is yours. She reads the card out loud and it will not match the drawing.
D364 (Thu Dec 30) The Table of Everybody
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Holly, yew and mistletoe berries are poisonous and Kreston mouths everything he picks up, so cut pine, spruce or ivy only and go through the bag before it comes indoors. No berries on that table at all. The battery tea light needs its screws done up tight, because a button battery swallowed at 18 months is a surgical emergency.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Naming Everybody Out Loud] **🧰 You need:** paper · 1 marker · a chair each.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Naming Everybody Out Loud] 1. Ask her to name everybody in the family. Write every name in the order she says it, and add nobody she has not named.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Naming Everybody Out Loud] 2. Read the list back. She remembers three more, one a friend from swimming and one somebody's dog.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Naming Everybody Out Loud] 3. Ask who is missing. The people who have died come up here, or at 9:15, and either is fine.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Photographs Standing Up] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Photographs Standing Up] 1. She picks the spot. A side table, a tray on a shelf, the end of the bookcase, out of the traffic and off the floor.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Photographs Standing Up] 2. Cloth over it. She straightens it until she is satisfied.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Photographs Standing Up] 3. One photo per name on her list. Anyone with no print, she draws on card.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Photographs Standing Up] 4. Fold each card strip into an L, tape a photo to the upright, stand it up. She says each name out loud.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Photographs Standing Up] 5. Marker under each stand: the name. She writes the letters she knows and you fill in the rest.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Photographs Standing Up] 6. Battery tea light in the middle, or the flashlight face up under a jar. No flame on this table, and she switches it on herself. The greenery from the 10:30 walk goes round the base at 11:15, once you are both home with it.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Cutting Green for the Table] Take a bag and cut a handful of green for the table. Pine and spruce are the
    [🌳 Get Outside: Cutting Green for the Table] easy ones at the end of December, and both smell of themselves when she
    [🌳 Get Outside: Cutting Green for the Table] crushes a needle. Ivy off a fence holds its shape for a week. Cut low, cut
    [🌳 Get Outside: Cutting Green for the Table] little, and ask before taking from a neighbor's yard.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Cutting Green for the Table] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Letters to the People in the Photos] The table is full of people who are somewhere else, and the mail still runs
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Letters to the People in the Photos] tomorrow.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Letters to the People in the Photos] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Letters to the People in the Photos] 1. Go round the table and pick out the living ones. Those are the letters. She will want to write to the others too, so say the truth: those stay on the table.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Letters to the People in the Photos] 2. One folded card per person, drawn in crayon first. She decides who gets which drawing.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Letters to the People in the Photos] 3. She dictates and you write it in pen, word for word, including the part that makes no sense.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Letters to the People in the Photos] 4. She picks a sticker to seal each envelope, one per person.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Letters to the People in the Photos] 5. Address them from your list. She puts the stamp on, top right corner, pressed with her thumb.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Letters to the People in the Photos] 6. The stack goes in her hands to the post office at 2:30, and she mails them one at a time.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Letters to the People in the Photos] 7. Anything she has not finished stays on the table for 4:00 and goes out tomorrow.
